## 1. [An Adaptive Reduced Speed of Light Approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21353) <a id="link1"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21353

**Authors:** Chong-Chong He (Australian National University, Australia), Mark R. Krumholz (Australian National University, Australia), Benjamin D. Wibking (The Observatories of the Carnegie Institution for Science, USA)

**Abstract:** The reduced speed of light approximation (RSLA) is a common approach in radiation-hydrodynamic (RHD) simulations of non-relativistic systems where the gas speed $v_{\rm gas} \ll c$. In the RSLA one eases the severe time step constraint imposed by the speed of light $c$ by replacing it with a reduced value $\hat{c}$ chosen so that $v_{\rm gas} \ll \hat{c} \ll c$. This approach works well in flows with low or moderate optical depth, but fails in optically thick media, where it makes thermal relaxation too slow and gives incorrect RHD wave modes. Here we propose an Adaptive Reduced Speed of Light Approximation (ARSA) that removes these problems by replacing the global speed reduction factor $\phi_0 \equiv \hat{c}/c$ with a local, cell-by-cell reduction factor $\phi$ derived from a von Neumann stability analysis of the implicit-explicit update used by modern two-moment RHD codes. The resulting limiter has no tunable parameters, can be computed using only local operations, and improves on the classic RSLA by allowing $\phi \to 1$ in cells where a reduced speed of light is not required because the exchange with the gas damps unstable modes, allowing recovery of the correct thermal relaxation and wave-damping rates in highly-opaque regions. We compare ARSA to the classic RSLA in a range of standard RHD test problems, and show that in opaque regions where gas holds most of the heat capacity the ARSA is much more accurate than the RSLA at the same time step, or that it can achieve the same accuracy as the RSLA with up to 100 times fewer time steps. However, gains are limited in regions where radiation holds a large share of the heat capacity, as it does in some astrophysical systems.

**Comment:** New radiation-hydrodynamics method for galaxy simulations

**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 2. [Model-Driven Stellar Spectroscopy with the Total Differential Spectral Model (TDSM): Framework and Its Application to 11 Million LAMOST Low-Resolution Spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20633) <a id="link2"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20633

**Authors:** Lanya Mou, Maosheng Xiang, Haibo Yuan, Tianmin Wu, Meng Zhang

**Abstract:** Deriving stellar astrophysical parameters from large-scale spectroscopic surveys requires accurate modeling of spectra across a wide wavelength coverage and a broad range of stellar parameter space, which remains a great challenge. In this work, we present \texttt{TDSM}, a physically motivated neural network architecture that self-consistently emulates both the flux and its first-order derivative of the ab initio stellar spectra. The method enables accurate and precise emulation of Kurucz synthetic spectra with severely blended features at $R \simeq 2000$ in a high-dimensional parameter space, achieving a typical pixel-wise flux precision of 0.05\% in the wavelength range $3700<\lambda<9000${\AA} over the parameter range of $3000 < T_{\rm eff} < 45,000$~K, $-1 < \log g < 5$, and $-4.0 < \mathrm{[Fe/H]} < 0.5$. The model is applied to LAMOST DR10 to derive stellar parameters for 11 million spectra spanning the full model parameter range. We conduct detailed refinement and calibration efforts to mitigate systematic errors, which are revealed to be significant and complex due to imperfections in the ab initio model atmosphere. The resultant catalog provides eight stellar labels -- $T_{\rm eff}$, $\log g$, $v_{\rm mic}$, [Fe/H], [$\alpha$/Fe], [C/Fe], [N/Fe], and [Mg/Fe] -- for 8 million unique stars (11 million spectra including repeat observations) across the full parameter space. This adds a valuable dataset for studies of Galactic structure and archaeology, stellar populations, and related areas of astrophysics.

**Comment:** New stellar atmosphere model emulation and bolometric parameter tables

**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 7

---

## 3. [The PHAROS Spectral Library: Data Processing, Spectral Reconstruction, and Uncertainty Propagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19734) <a id="link3"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.19734

**Authors:** Viktor Yuri Doná Sumida

**Abstract:** We document the data reduction, reconstruction choices, and available uncertainty propagation underlying fifteen PHAROS stellar spectral energy distributions from 0.5 to 5000 nm. A catalogue timeline and a practical FITS guide introduce the available products, their column and extension conventions, and examples of reading, integration, and uncertainty display. Shared methods are presented once, including flux geometry, support-aware integration, X-ray likelihoods, the 2025 X/EUV luminosity relation, intrinsic Ly$\alpha$ inference, airglow treatment, spectral junctions, and covariance-aware resampling. Target sections record the adopted observations and alternatives, including spatially separated binary components, unresolved system-level EUV measurements, direct EUVE constraints, optical stellar-twin transfer, and literature-anchored cases where new spectral inference is unsupported. The distinction between native measurement errors, conditional model intervals, empirical predictive scatter, and physical-method sensitivity is maintained. Signed measurements and nonnegative irradiation products are distinguished. Each target section concludes with the final nominal spectrum and its available uncertainty bounds on common axes. This library provides the detailed provenance and interpretation needed to accompany the catalogue paper and its machine-readable products.

**Comment:** New stellar atmosphere spectral library with uncertainty propagation

**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 4. [Orion I: Discovery of an Isolated dE,N Galaxy at 60 Mph](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19631) <a id="link4"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.19631

**Authors:** W. E. Harris, T. Carleton, R. Ortiz, L. Furtak, J. Berkheimer, R. Honor, B. Frye, B. Holwerda, C. Willmer, R. Windhorst

**Abstract:** Inspection of deep JWST images centered on the lensing cluster of galaxies MACS J0454.1-0300 reveals a small, well resolved stellar system that is clearly in the foreground. Photometric measurements including a color-magnitude diagram and radial profile show that it is a nucleated dwarf early-type galaxy (dE,N) at a distance of ~60 Mpc and with structurally normal properties. This object (J04:54:07.39-02:59:22.5, referred to as Orion I for shorthand) may be an outlying member of a previously known small galaxy group, or may be simply an isolated field galaxy depending on the accuracy of its distance estimate. The discovery of Orion I suggests that other small stellar systems in the nearby universe might be found through a search of the many deep \jwst imaging fields that are now being accumulated.

**Comment:** Dwarf early-type galaxy discovery, CMD and radial profile

**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 5. [Initial Conditions Dictate Gas-Stellar Counter-Rotation in Galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19294) <a id="link5"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.19294

**Authors:** Lin Zhu, Hao-Ran Yu, Min Bao, Ming-Jie Sheng, Jian Qin, Shuai Lu, Min Du, Yanmei Chen, Yipeng Jing

**Abstract:** We present a physical origin of gas-stellar counter-rotation in galaxies. Scale-dependent partitions of tidal torques from cosmic primordial perturbations source angular momentum for distinct baryonic components. This scale dependence maps to radial hierarchy in Lagrangian space clustering: stellar components, assembled at earlier times from the inner protohalo, retain persistent memory of small-scale tidal torques, whereas gas, accreted later from outer Lagrangian regions, is governed by large-scale perturbations and can develop counter-rotation. This mechanism is observationally testable and offers a direct probe of the hierarchical cold dark matter (CDM) paradigm. Unlike conventional late-time scenarios that only explain the existence of counter-rotation, our framework further predicts its component-resolved direction, enabling component-resolved angular momentum to serve as a new probe of primordial density perturbations and improving cosmological initial condition reconstruction.

**Relevance:** 6
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 6. [Unbound matter in the circum-halo medium: a common mediator of halo secondary bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19491) <a id="link6"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.19491

**Authors:** Qinglin Ma, Yangyao Chen, Houjun Mo

**Abstract:** Halo secondary bias describes the dependence of halo clustering on properties beyond mass. Here we report that a new environmental metric, the unbound matter fraction ($f_{\rm un}$) in the $2$--$3\,R_{\rm vir}$ shell around a halo, can largely account for secondary bias. Using haloes with $M_{\rm vir}=10^{10.5}$--$10^{14.5}h^{-1}{\rm M_\odot}$ at $z=0$ obtained from TNG300-1-Dark, we find that controlling for $f_{\rm un}$ reduces the secondary bias of formation time, concentration, spin, shape and velocity anisotropy to a residual correlation of $\lesssim 4\%$, outperforming other environmental metrics such as the tidal anisotropy for spin and velocity anisotropy. Applying a stacking method to the circum-halo medium (CHM), we identify two distinct flow patterns: low-$f_{\rm un}$ haloes co-move with coherent flows, with the CHM converging onto the haloes; in contrast, high-$f_{\rm un}$ haloes move through dynamically hot, partially virialized structures, with the CHM passing by rather than being accreted. This dichotomy points to two distinct regimes of secondary bias, a `cold' regime and a `hot' regime, in which tidal and inertial forces act as the main drivers, respectively, with the dominant mechanism migrating from the former to the latter as haloes assemble into massive structures. At fixed large-scale environment, $f_{\rm un}$ further indicates the presence of nearby neighbours and the competitive accretion. Our results highlight the role of unbound matter as a mediator of environmental effects on haloes, and reveal the physical mechanisms that shape the CHM pattern through which these effects are mediated.

**Relevance:** 4
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 7. [On the origin of Milky Way binary black holes with LISA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21337) <a id="link7"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21337

**Authors:** Charles Dalang, Enrique Gaztanaga

**Abstract:** Primordial black holes (PBHs) may constitute a fraction of dark matter, but distinguishing them from black holes of stellar origin remains a major challenge. We propose a new method based on the three-dimensional localization of Galactic Binary Black Holes (BBHs) by the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA). While stellar-origin BBHs are expected to be associated with the stellar distribution of the Milky Way, PBHs may trace its more extended dark matter halo. We introduce a spatial overlap ratio that compares the gravitational-wave localization of each binary with the normalized stellar and dark matter density profiles. Using a Fisher-matrix forecast and 1000 Monte Carlo realizations of a 10-year LISA mission, we find that detecting three BBHs with an overlap ratio ln B < 0 occurs in approximately 99% of simulations where BBHs trace dark matter, but in fewer than 0.1% of simulations where they follow the stellar distribution, even when including a stellar halo containing 3% of the Galactic stellar mass. These results suggest that the localization of only a few Galactic BBHs could provide strong evidence for a primordial population, offering an independent test of their possible contribution to dark matter.

**Relevance:** 4
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 8. [Unexpected Plateau: The Constant $ \rm \log \epsilon (Th/Eu)$ Ratio of Actinide-Boost Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21350) <a id="link8"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21350

**Authors:** J. Shejeelammal, Asa Skuladottir, Marta Molero, Heitor Ernandes, Jan Kuske, Almudena Arcones, Raghubar Singh

**Abstract:** The astrophysical site of the rapid neutron-capture process ($r$-process) is still one of the major open questions in astronomy. A key mystery is the origin of the peculiar actinide-to-lanthanide ratio found in the so called actinide-boost stars, with $ \rm \log \epsilon (Th/Eu)>-0.35$. Here we present the largest sample of Th measurements in stars formed in an external galaxy, Gaia Sausage Enceladus. From this sample, we present the three most metal-rich actinide-boost stars discovered to date, $\rm -1.6<[Fe/H]<-1.1$. In addition, we measured Th in a comparison sample of Milky Way stars formed in situ, and compiled Th measurements from the literature which do not account for non-local thermodynamic equilibrium (NLTE) effects. After applying NLTE corrections to the combined sample, a bimodality in the $\rm\log\epsilon (Th/Eu)$ ratio is revealed. Unexpectedly, actinide-boost stars lie on a very narrow $(\sigma=0.02)$ plateau of $\rm \log\epsilon(Th/Eu)=-0.2$, clearly separated from actinide-normal stars. Most (if not all) of the actinide-boost stars in our sample originate from outside the Milky Way. Chemical evolution models show that such a plateau can arise, even at [Fe/H] = $-$1, when a single actinide-rich event dominates the r-process content of a small or gas-depleted system. Combined with other evidence, our results suggest that mergers with neutron stars are the most promising astrophysical site for actinide-boost stars.

**Relevance:** 4
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 9. [Orbital decomposition and evolution of LEGA-C-like galaxies in IllustrisTNG from z = 1 to z = 0](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20884) <a id="link9"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20884

**Authors:** Prem Kumar, Alina Boecker, Glenn van de Ven, Stefanie Reiter, Ling Zhu, Annalisa Pillepich, Prashin Jethwa, Sabine Thater

**Abstract:** Stellar orbits encode the dynamical history of galaxies and provide a physically motivated link between galaxy structures and assembly processes. Although orbit-based decompositions are well established in the Local Universe, their evolution across cosmic time, especially at higher redshifts, remains poorly explored. We investigate the orbital structure of \texttt{LEGA-C-like} galaxies in the \texttt{TNG100} simulation from $z=1$ to $z=0$ to identify how stellar mass, star formation activity, environment, merger history, gas content, and ex situ stellar mass growth shape their orbital transformation. We decomposed the stellar orbital structure of simulated galaxies into cold, warm, hot, and counter-rotating (CR) components using time-averaged orbital circularities. We find a strong mass dependence of orbital structure at $z=0$ as well as at $z=1$. Galaxies below $\log(M_\star/M_\odot)\sim11.2$ have a combined warm and cold orbital fraction of $\sim70\%$, whereas more massive systems have a hot-orbit fraction of $\sim60\%$. These trends are consistent with assembly pathways driven predominantly by wet and dry major mergers, respectively. Tracking the same galaxies to $z=0$ reveals a systematic, but mass-dependent, evolution toward lower rotational support: At both low and high stellar masses, the hot-orbit fraction increases, whereas the relative change is greater in low-mass galaxies. The survival of cold structure depends strongly on the star formation state at $z=1$; star-forming progenitors retain substantially larger cold fractions than the quiescent progenitors. At both redshifts, the orbital fractions show a rise (decline) in hot and CR (cold and warm) orbits with the ex situ stellar mass fraction at a fixed stellar mass. These results provide the first evolutionary framework for interpreting orbit-based decompositions of galaxies at the \texttt{LEGA-C} epoch.

## 10. [The LAMOST Galaxy Parameter Catalog (LAGC): Stellar Populations, Kinematics, and Emission Lines of Galaxies in the LAMOST Spectroscopic Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20917) <a id="link10"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20917

**Authors:** Jia-Feng Lu, Shi-Yin Shen, Qian Liu, Shuai Feng, Yong Shi

**Abstract:** The LAMOST Galaxy Parameter Catalog (LAGC) is a value-added data product of the LAMOST spectroscopic survey, generated by an automated spectral-fitting pipeline and released as a continuously updated catalogue series. Despite the vast data volume of the LAMOST spectroscopic survey, no dedicated, homogeneous pipeline had previously been able to extract stellar population, kinematic, and emission-line information simultaneously from its galaxy spectra, primarily because of the poorly controlled absolute flux calibration and a line spread function (LSF) that varies with wavelength, fibre, and time. The pipeline addresses both challenges through the SEW full-spectrum fitting method, which operates in equivalent-width space and is therefore immune to multiplicative flux-calibration errors, together with an accurate, wavelength-dependent model of the LAMOST LSF that enables unbiased kinematic measurements. As the pilot release of the catalogue series, we process the full galaxy sample of the latest LAMOST data release (283,691 spectra) and measure stellar population parameters, stellar and gas kinematics, and equivalent widths of 19 emission lines, each with bootstrap-based uncertainties. Repeat LAMOST observations confirm that the measurements are reproducible and that the bootstrap uncertainties are well calibrated, while comparison with SDSS shows only minor systematic offsets, attributable mainly to the SDSS LSF modelling. The measurements reproduce the well-established relations of the local galaxy population, and the catalogue will be publicly available and continuously updated as new observations become available. LAGC thus provides a reliable scientific catalogue for galaxy evolution studies, while the methodology behind it offers a blueprint for the next generation of fibre-fed spectroscopic surveys.

**Novelty:** 5

## 11. [Gravitational Potential of CALIFA Galaxies from the Blue Cloud to the Red Sequence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20940) <a id="link11"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20940

**Authors:** Veselina Kalinova

**Abstract:** We explore the gravitational potential of 238 (E1$-$Sdm) CALIFA galaxies from the Blue cloud to the Red Sequence using the star formation rate$-$stellar mass diagram ($\mathrm{SFR}-M_\star$) diagnosis. On average, galaxies in the Blue cloud possess slow-rising circular velocity curves (CVCs), those in the Green valley have flat CVCs, while galaxies in the Red sequence are characterised with peaked CVCs. Both the maximum circular velocity ($V_{c,\mathrm{max}}$) and asymptotic circular velocity ($V_{c,\mathrm{flat}}$) of the galaxies increase from the Blue cloud to the Red sequence, and show approximately linear relations with the logarithmic offsets from the star-forming main sequence ($\Delta\mathrm{SFR}$s). The $\Delta\mathrm{SFR}-\log_{10}(V_{c,\mathrm{max}})$ relation has a steeper slope ($-0.154 \pm 0.012$) than the $\Delta\mathrm{SFR}-\log_{10}(V_{c,\mathrm{flat}})$ relation ($-0.124 \pm 0.011$). These results suggest that $V_{c,\mathrm{max}}$, tracing the inner gravitational potential, indicates a stronger variation along the quenching parameter $\Delta\mathrm{SFR}$ than $V_{c,\mathrm{flat}}$, which is more strongly influenced by the outer mass distribution and dark matter halo.

## 12. [Interpreting JWST UV luminosity functions and angular correlation functions via a "universal language" for high-redshift galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18850) <a id="link12"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18850

**Authors:** Ivan Nikolić, Andrei Mesinger, Steven G. Murray

**Abstract:** The James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) found more bright $z\gtrsim10$ galaxies than expected before its launch. No consensus has emerged on whether difficulties in reproducing them stem from missing physics, simplifying assumptions that break down at the highest redshifts, or model mis-calibration. This is complicated because most galaxy models cannot be compared like-to-like, and models invoking different physical processes can make similar predictions for compressed summaries such as ultraviolet luminosity functions (UV LFs). Here we describe galaxy populations in a universal language: a chain of physically and empirically motivated conditional distributions linking halo mass, stellar mass, star formation rate and UV magnitude, defined consistently across models and data. We build a broad prior from fits to five hydrodynamic simulations, which predict very different high-redshift galaxy populations. We infer the parameters of this flexible galaxy model from $z>6$ JWST observations of UV LFs and angular correlation functions (ACFs). Including the mass-complete ACFs disfavors a large scatter around the stellar-to-halo mass relation (SHMR). With simulation-based priors, the star-forming main sequence (SFMS) is described by a star-formation timescale of about one halo dynamical time, and the scatter around the SFMS increases toward lower stellar masses. The SFMS scatter is the main source of stochasticity in UV magnitude at fixed halo mass, accounting for a median $\sim$61% of the variance at $M_{\rm UV}=-20$ (SHMR scatter: $\sim$36%). Bayesian model selection finds no evidence for redshift evolution of the SHMR or for an SFMS slope different from unity. At $z\sim20$, our fiducial model predicts UV LFs orders of magnitude below estimates from photometric candidates. A consensus on the discrepant UV LF estimates at $z\geq14$ is needed to determine whether model extensions are required.

**Novelty:** 6

## 13. [A COMPASS to the Galaxy: Joint nucleosynthetic yield set comparison and parameter inference in Galactic chemical evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21175) <a id="link13"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21175

**Authors:** Berkay Günes, Giuseppe Viterbo, Sven Buder, Tobias Buck

**Abstract:** Galactic Chemical Evolution (GCE) models connect stellar nucleosynthesis to observed abundance patterns, but their interpretation requires simultaneous parameter inference and comparison between competing stellar yield prescriptions. This remains challenging due to intractable likelihoods and growing dataset complexity. Using compass, a novel simulation-based inference framework, we perform, for the first time, amortized joint Bayesian model comparison and parameter inference for GCE models with intractable likelihoods within a unified architecture. Compass employs a conditional score-based diffusion model with a transformer architecture to learn both the posterior and likelihood directly from forward simulations, enabling efficient inference and likelihood-based ranking of competing nucleosynthetic yield sets. In controlled tests, compass identifies the generating yield set and recovers ground-truth parameters with accuracy comparable to existing simulation-based inference (SBI) methods, while reducing computational cost by orders of magnitude. Applied to Milky Way data, the framework favors a hybrid NuGrid + IllustrisTNG yield prescription, confirmed by an independent classifier, and infers an initial mass function slope of $\alpha_{\rm IMF} = -2.65 \pm 0.02$ and a SN$_{\rm Ia}$ normalization of $\log(N_{\rm Ia}) = -2.81 \pm 0.02$, with a systematic spread of $\approx 0.3$ in $\alpha_{\rm IMF}$ between the best-ranked yield sets, consistent with independent constraints. The attention mechanism confirms that the inferred parameters are driven by physically meaningful abundance signatures. By unifying amortized parameter inference, Bayesian model comparison, and interpretability in a single probabilistic framework, compass provides a scalable approach for precision chemical evolution studies with current and forthcoming spectroscopic surveys.

**Novelty:** 6

## 14. [The mass density slope of early-type galaxies: newest results from the redshift distribution of strong gravitational lenses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20141) <a id="link14"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20141

**Authors:** Xinyue Jiang, Shuo Cao, Wuzheng Guo, Chenfa Zheng, Chengsheng Mu, Shuaibo Geng, Marek Biesiada, Yalong Nan

**Abstract:** The mass distribution of early-type galaxies (ETGs) encodes key information about baryonic processes and dark-matter assembly. In this paper, we propose a new framework to investigate the mass-density profiles of ETGs and their evolution with redshift, based on the redshift distribution of strong gravitational lenses. We compile 200 galaxy-scale strong lensing systems with complete information, including lens and source redshifts, Einstein radii, and spectroscopic velocity dispersions. To account for the heterogeneous construction of the compiled sample, we model a lens-redshift-dependent selection function for each survey category. By combining the lens-redshift distribution with velocity dispersion function (VDF) and spherical Jeans-based mapping, we obtain population-level constraints on the total mass-density slope gamma in three lens-redshift intervals, marginalizing over the luminosity-density slope delta and the stellar orbital anisotropy beta. We find that the total mass density of ETGs remains close to isothermal at low and intermediate redshifts ($\gamma=2.00^{+0.07}_{-0.15}$ at 0 < z_l < 0.5 and $\gamma=1.96^{+0.16}_{-0.26}$ at 0.5 < z_l < 0.6), with a shallower effective slope at higher redshift ($\gamma=1.81^{+0.17}_{-0.21}$ at $0.6<z_l\lesssim1.0$). The marginalized posteriors of delta and beta remain broad, consistent with $\delta\simeq2$ and with isotropic orbits in all three redshift intervals. These results are consistent with the redshift trend predicted by Horizon-AGN simulations, in which active galactic nucleus (AGN) feedback, together with stellar assembly and merging, contributes to the structural evolution of ETGs. Applied to the much larger lens samples expected from future missions such as the Chinese Space Station Telescope (CSST), this method will enable tighter tests of density-slope evolution and its connection to galaxy formation processes.

## 15. [Inferring accretion bursts in intermediate-mass protostars from asteroseismic imprints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20154) <a id="link15"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20154

**Authors:** Johannes J{ø}rgensen, Eduard Vorobyov, Konstanze Zwintz, Ebraheem Farag, Adolfo Carvalho, Jan Henneco

**Abstract:** During star formation, objects may gain the majority of their final mass through regular and intense bursts of mass accretion. For bursts of the FU Orionis-type (FUor), accretion rates can climb to $10^{-3} \ \text{M}_\odot \text{yr}^{-1}$ during short windows of time, amounting to just a few percent of the main accretion phase. To study the occurrence of such events observationally, signatures of the current accretion bursts and tracers of recent bursts are crucial. We investigate the potential to identify accretion bursts in protostars and pre-main sequence stars of intermediate mass using asteroseismology. We use the stellar structure and evolution code MESA to evolve stellar models from the protostellar seed phase of $0.01 \ \text{M}_\odot$ and $1.5 \ \text{R}_\odot$ up to $4.0 \ \text{M}_\odot$ with a constant accretion rate of $10^{-6} \ \text{M}_\odot \text{yr}^{-1}$. A single accretion burst is initialized during the accretion phase. The stellar oscillation code GYRE is employed to compute the resulting asteroseismic properties of the models. Accretion bursts of up to $10^{-3} \ \text{M}_\odot \text{yr}^{-1}$ are sufficient to cause the convective cores of the models to recede, leaving behind a chemical trace which manifests itself in the form of quasi-periodic dips in the g-mode period spacing. This effect is strongest during the outburst, but also lingers for up to 475 years before the convective core is re-established and the dips are gone. Our experiment shows that a single accretion burst leaves an imprint in the deep interiors of protostars and pre-MS stars of intermediate mass, and that this imprint can be captured with asteroseismology. Our results are most conclusive for burst magnitudes greater than $1\times10^{-4} \ \text{M}_\odot \text{yr}^{-1}$, corresponding to FUor-type bursts.

**Novelty:** 5

## 16. [LAMOST III Low-resolution Spectroscopic Survey: Overview and Target Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20828) <a id="link16"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20828

**Authors:** Shuai Xu, Haibo Yuan, Maosheng Xiang, Bingqiu Chen, Yang Huang, Chun Wang, Hailong Yuan, Tianqi Cang, Xuefei Chen, Yuqin Chen, Yao-Quan Chu, Wenyuan Cui, Subo Dong, Min Fang, Shuai Feng, Jianning Fu, Wei-Min Gu, Yan-Xin Guo, Zhanwen Han, Jinliang Hou, Wen Hou, Bowen Huang, Zhiying Huo, Xiao Kong, Xu Kong, Guangwei Li, Haining Li, Jiangdan Li, Jiao Li, Chao Liu, Jifeng Liu, Qichun Liu, Xiao-Wei Liu, Xue Lu, Ali Luo, Zexi Niu, Dan Qiu, Shiyin Shen, Jianrong Shi, Botian Tang, Hao Tian, Haijun Tian, Zhijia Tian, Jian-Min Wang, Jie Wang, Xiaofeng Wang, Yuanchang Wang, Hong Wu, Jianghua Wu, Xue-Bing Wu, Qiqi Xia, Kai Xiao, Jiwei Xie, Hongliang Yan, Lin Yang, Xiaohu Yang, Haotong Zhang, Huawei Zhang, Hui Zhang, Liyun Zhang, Meng Zhang, Zhi-Xiang Zhang, Gang Zhao, Gong-Bo Zhao, He Zhao, Yong-Heng Zhao, Zhenya Zheng, Jing Zhong, Weikai Zong

**Abstract:** We present an overview of the LAMOST III Low-resolution Spectroscopic Survey (LRS), including its scientific goals, target selection strategy, and current observing status. Targets in the main survey are selected via weighted random sampling, using the inverse local density in the Hertzsprung-Russell (HR) diagram and in the G-magnitude distribution, with additional enhancement in selected regions of the HR diagram. The main survey is complemented by 35 dedicated add-on catalogs (including very metal-poor star candidates, halo stars, hot/young stars, wide binaries, extragalactic sources and so on) and a time-domain program providing multi-epoch spectroscopy of binaries and other peculiar systems. LAMOST III LRS is designed to map the structure and evolution of the Galactic disk, reconstruct the assembly and early chemical-enrichment history of the Galactic halo, improve stellar-parameter calibration, and characterize rare and variable objects. By June 2025, the LAMOST III LRS had obtained 1.53 million low-resolution spectra from 658 plates, corresponding to 1,264,052 unique sources. Relative to LAMOST I+II, the current sample is enriched in O/B stars, subgiants, main-sequence turn-off stars, metal-poor stars, and other previously underrepresented populations. In particular, the fractions of metal-poor and very metal-poor stars increase from 3.8% and 0.4% in LAMOST I+II to 9.0% and 1.1% in LAMOST III LRS, respectively, demonstrating the improved efficiency of the revised target-selection strategy for Galactic archaeology and stellar astrophysics.

## 17. [Ca II IRT activity indices from $Gaia$ spectroscopy: a baseline method for photospheric contribution estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20636) <a id="link17"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20636

**Authors:** Wang Song, Li Tanda, Han Henggeng, Li Xue, Liu Jifeng

**Abstract:** The $Gaia$ high-resolution spectroscopic observations using Radial Velocity Spectrometer offers a unique opportunity to study stellar Ca II infrared triplet (IRT) activity. A key step in estimating activity indices is subtracting the photospheric contribution. The canonical template-based method often fails because, for many stars, the observed line cores are deeper than the model predictions, which prevents valid activity measurements. In this work, we explore an alternative approach that constructs a baseline from less active stars as a function of effective temperature (and metallicity) to represent the photospheric contribution. Our results show that, for dwarf stars, compared with the canonical method, the baseline method not only yields far more objects with valid activity measurements, but also avoids many extremely low and unreliable values (e.g., log$R'_{\rm IRT} \lesssim -6$). The Ca II IRT activity indices derived from the baseline method correlate well with other activity proxies, confirming their reliability as activity indicators. Furthermore, they show a relation with stellar rotation, not only recovering the typical rotation--activity relation, but also revealing fine structures that may be associated with different regimes in gyrochronology. Our findings suggest that the baseline method can serve as a robust alternative for deriving accurate activity estimates, and will be particularly useful for obtaining activity indices for tens of millions of stars with the upcoming $Gaia$ Data Release 4.

**Novelty:** 5

## 18. [Unveiling the Multi-Phase Outflow in NGC 4151 with JWST Observations - I. Integrated Properties of the Outflow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20236) <a id="link18"></a>

**Authors:** Léa M. Feuillet, Steve Kraemer, Anna Trindade Falcão, Travis C. Fischer, Mitchell Revalski, Lee Armus, Riccardo Middei, James N. Reeves, Valentina Braito

**Abstract:** The biconical outflow of the nearby Seyfert 1.5 AGN NGC 4151 has been studied across the electromagnetic spectrum, yet mapping its multiphase structure with a single facility has remained challenging. Leveraging the broad wavelength coverage of the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST), we present new near- and mid-infrared observations tracing the ionization stratification of these outflows with a unified dataset. We analyze emission lines originating from molecular gas to highly ionized plasma and analyze the ionized gas using a 5-component Cloudy photoionization model covering three primary phases. Applying the footprint line method to characterize the X-ray emitting gas, we find that it holds the largest share of the total mass, suggesting that it is a primary candidate for driving AGN feedback in this system.

**Authors:** Jessica M. Berkheimer, Rogier A. Windhorst, William E. Harris, Timothy Carleton, Rafael Ortiz III, S. P. Willner

**Abstract:** We present a study of the compact-source population associated with the massive galaxy cluster MACS J1149.5+2223 at z=0.544 using deep JWST/NIRCam imaging in F115W, F150W, and F200W. After photometric and morphological selection, we identify 1,202 unresolved compact-source candidates. The observed luminosity distribution samples only the bright side of the expected globular-cluster luminosity function (GCLF), remaining several magnitudes above the turnover. Based on published GCLF turnovers for Abell 2744 and MACS 0417, we adopt an expected turnover magnitude of m_0=33.66 mag for MACS 1149. A forward-modeled fit then yields sigma=1.71 mag, which characterizes the observed bright-side slope under the adopted turnover rather than the intrinsic width of the full GCLF. Comparison with PARSEC simple stellar population models shows that the elevated luminosities of the compact sources are consistent with the expected fading of younger stellar populations as they evolve toward the present epoch. The brightest sources reach luminosities comparable to those of ultra-compact dwarfs. However, stellar-population degeneracies and possible foreground contamination make it difficult to distinguish. Together with recent JWST studies of globular cluster systems across a range of redshifts, MACS 1149 extends the emerging observational sequence of compact stellar systems and supports stellar population fading as an important contributor to their luminosity evolution across cosmic time.

Förster-Schreiber, R. Genzel, S. Gillessen, P. Grani, M. Hartl, K. Kravchenko, D. Lutz, M. Macintosh, T. Ott, D. Pearson, A. Puglisi, S. Rabien, C. Rau, A. Riccardi, T. Shimizu, E. Sturm, L. Tacconi, M. Xompero

**Abstract:** Classical Cepheids (CCs) are standard candles and key tracers of young stellar populations. However, their use in the most obscured regions is hampered by the high interstellar extinction at optical wavelengths. Near-infrared spectroscopy offers major advantages by greatly reducing absorption corrections and enabling access to regions of the Milky Way that are still poorly sampled. In this work, we determine the atmospheric parameters and abundances of Fe, Mg and Si for a sample of 27 CCs based on observations with ERIS@VLT in J-band low-resolution mode ($R \sim 4000$). Combining PySME with 1D MARCS atmosphere models and non local thermal equilibrium grids, we simultaneous optimise the atmospheric parameters through spectral fitting. This method is validated with a direct comparison with high-resolution optical investigations and re-analysis of archive optical spectra for the targets in common. The results show good consistency in both metallicity and chemical abundances. We obtain mean abundance ratios of $\mathrm{\langle[Mg/Fe]\rangle=0.01\pm0.12~dex}$ and $\mathrm{\langle[Si/Fe]\rangle=0.10\pm0.09~dex}$, in agreement with reference samples. Moreover, we provide for the first time the Mg and Si abundances of 12 CCs. Finally, we have found distinct kinematic properties for two of the most distant Galactic CCs, located at Galactocentric distances of $\mathrm{\approx 28-29~kpc}$ and with extreme values of vertical height, $\mathrm{\approx +4.8~kpc}$ and $\mathrm{\approx -5.3~kpc}$; more data are required to constrain their nature. Our results confirm that J-band spectroscopy at low-resolution can be used as a good diagnostic for the chemical investigation in highly obscured Galactic regions, otherwise inaccessible at optical wavelengths.

## 21. [The Interplay of Stellar Feedback and Gravity in the Wat01-S208 Star-forming Complex](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20967) <a id="link21"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20967

**Authors:** Harmeen Kaur, Saurabh Sharma, Lokesh K. Dewangan, Devendra K. Ojha, Alok Durgapal, Bhuwan C. Bhatt, Aayushi Verma, Tarak Chand, Kartik Gokhe, Mamta, Manojit Chakraborty

**Abstract:** To investigate the processes governing clustered star formation, we present a multi-wavelength study of the star-forming complex hosting clusters Waterloo 01 and [BDS2003]64 in Sh 2-208. Both clusters exhibit elongated morphologies, with radii of $\sim1.5$ and $\sim0.9$ pc, respectively, and are located at a common distance of $\sim3.5$ kpc. Wat01 displays hierarchical structure ($Q=0.6$) and signature of mass segregation ($\Gamma_{\rm MSR}=1.5\pm0.6$). Its relaxation time, comparable to or longer than its age, suggests that the observed segregation may have a primordial contribution. The mass function follows a single power law, $\Gamma=-1.62\pm0.15$, over $\sim0.7$-$19.6$ M$_\odot$. Molecular-line observations reveal distinct velocity components associated with S208 and Wat01, indicating different molecular environments within the complex. Wat01 is strongly shaped by massive-star feedback, with an evacuated cavity, surrounding shell, and compressed globules consistent with an expanding H\,{\sc ii} region; feedback may have promoted localized star formation, although a direct causal connection cannot be established. In contrast, \textit{Herschel} data uncover a hub-filament system (HFS) in S208, with the central hub associated with BDS64 and the H\,{\sc ii} region powered by 'S1'. The filaments remain relatively well-preserved, indicating that gravity continues to play an important role in assembling dense gas toward the hub and in clustered star formation, even as feedback begins to affect the immediate surroundings. Overall, this complex illustrates the coexistence of feedback-processed and comparatively less-disrupted star-forming environments, highlighting the evolving interplay between stellar feedback and gravity in clustered star formation.

Uhlemann (Fakultät für Physik, Universität Bielefeld, Postfach 100131, 33501 Bielefeld, Germany), C. Valieri (INFN-Sezione di Bologna, Viale Berti Pichat 6/2, 40127 Bologna, Italy), A. Vergani (INAF-Osservatorio di Astrofisica e Scienza dello Spazio di Bologna, Via Piero Gobetti 93/3, 40129 Bologna, Italy), G. Vernardos (Department of Physics and Astronomy, Lehman College of the CUNY, Bronx, NY 10468, USA, American Museum of Natural History, Department of Astrophysics, New York, NY 10024, USA), S. Vinciguerra (Aix-Marseille Université, CNRS, CNES, LAM, Marseille, France), M. von Wietersheim-Kramsta (Department of Physics, Institute for Computational Cosmology, Durham University, South Road, Durham, DH1 3LE, UK, Department of Physics, Centre for Extragalactic Astronomy, Durham University, South Road, Durham, DH1 3LE, UK), N. A. Walton (Institute of Astronomy, University of Cambridge, Madingley Road, Cambridge CB3 0HA, UK), A. H. Wright (Ruhr University Bochum, Faculty of Physics and Astronomy, Astronomical Institute)

**Abstract:** Strong gravitational-lensing systems with multiple source planes are powerful tools for probing the stellar and dark matter content of galaxies. Lens modelling can measure the relative Einstein radii of the two source planes, which encode a ratio of angular-diameter distances determined by the lens and source redshifts and the dark energy equation of state, independently of the Hubble constant. Galaxy-scale double-source-plane lenses (DSPLs) are extremely rare. We present a sample of 84 galaxy-scale DSPL candidates in Euclid Data Release 1 (DR1), of which 80 are newly identified. These systems were selected using machine-learning lens-finding models followed by visual inspection by citizen scientists and experts. We then used lens modelling to test the DSPL interpretation. Among our lens candidates, 11 form the 'Gold' sample: each shows two sets of multiple images at different radii, and the observed configuration is reproduced by a DSPL lens model. The remaining 73 'Silver' candidates have at least two lensed arcs from visual inspection, but either lack multiple images for at least one arc, have a best-fitting DSPL distance ratio close to the single-source-plane limit, remain compatible with a single-plane model with multiple sources, or have not yet been modelled. Even if all 84 candidates are DSPLs, the sample falls below the approximately 90 systems expected from extrapolating the Euclid Quick Data Release 1 (Q1) yield and the approximately 200 obtained by scaling the LensPop forecast to the DR1 area, indicating that many DSPLs remain undetected in DR1. Spectroscopy or higher-resolution imaging is required before we can be confident that the Silver candidates are DSPLs.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

## 23. [Gaia as a 0.1 arcsec point-source survey across the sky: dynamical scene reconstruction from one-dimensional scans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21510) <a id="link23"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21510

**Authors:** Sahar Shahaf, David W. Hogg, Kareem El-Badry, Hans-Walter Rix, René Andrae

**Abstract:** Gaia has observed every detected source on the sky 50 to 100 times, each time through a window of about 1 by 2 arcseconds at a different scan angle. The resulting one-dimensional flux profiles to be released in Gaia DR4 encode the two-dimensional arrangement of nearby point sources at ~0.1 arcsecond resolution. Repeated observations also constrain their relative motion. We present a method to model the scene with a mixture of moving point sources. Complementing Gaia DPAC's source-environment reconstruction, our approach focuses on close, blended companions and models their relative motion. We first fit each window with a few point sources and obtain their along-scan positions. We then triangulate these positions from different scan angles to identify candidate sources on the sky. A joint forward model of all windows measures positions, fluxes, relative proper motions, and parallax differences, even for blended components. In simulations, we recover companions at separations smaller than the instrumental profile width. For a moving companion at ~75 mas separation with a flux ratio of ~0.2, we obtain a separation precision of ~0.1 mas and a relative proper-motion precision of ~0.1 mas/yr per component, even in a scene contaminated by a third source. These precisions assume a known instrumental profile and independent Gaussian noise. We also recover differential parallax for a chance-aligned source. Modeling nearby contaminants prevents significant biases. With a Gaia-like instrumental profile, sampling and scan law, we show a sensitivity of ~5 mag in contrast at separations of 50-150 mas, and we discuss its dependence on the color term of Gaia's line-spread function. The method could extend the census of close companions and provide relative astrometry, and so dynamical information, along short orbital arcs. External measurements could also guide retrospective searches in Gaia's windows.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

## 24. [Gravitational Waves from Population III Black Hole Mergers in Primordial Gas Disks: Constraining Astrophysical Scenarios with the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna and the Einstein Telescope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21580) <a id="link24"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21580

**Authors:** Maulik Bhatt, Lucio Mayer, Pedro R. Capelo, Simona Gallerani, Andrea Pallottini

**Abstract:** The first generation of metal-free Population III (Pop~III) stars provides a possible formation channel for the black hole (BH) seeds in the early Universe. Their remnants may grow through gas accretion and mergers, producing gravitational wave (GW) signals detectable by future observatories. We develop a semi-analytical model of fragmentation in a primordial self-gravitating gas disk within minihaloes at $z > 15$, motivated by high-resolution cosmological simulations. The resulting Pop~III stars form around a central primary, some leaving massive BH remnants that migrate towards the central BH through dynamical friction from gas and dark matter, while the central BH grows through accretion and mergers. For a $10^6~{\rm M_\odot}$ halo at $z=20$, we explore how the initial mass function (IMF) and BH accretion efficiency affect detectability with the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) and the Einstein Telescope (ET). We find that both detectors can potentially reveal mergers of Pop III remnant BHs, but favor different astrophysical conditions. LISA pinpoints more massive central BHs ($\sim10^3$-$10^4 {\rm M_\odot}$), grown rapidly through mutual mergers alongside efficient gas accretion ($f_{\rm Edd}=1$), and a relatively top-heavy IMF ($\alpha=0.8$). In contrast, detectability with ET favors lower-mass BHs, with less efficient accretion ($f_{\rm Edd}=0.01$) and a steeper IMF ($\alpha=1.5$). These results highlight the potential of LISA and ET to probe Pop~III BH formation, growth, and mergers across different regions of parameter spaces, jointly constraining Pop~III star formation and early BH seed evolution.

## 25. [Quantifying the Dominance and Emergence of Brightest Cluster Galaxies in SPT and Planck Clusters: A Novel Application of the Gini Index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18929) <a id="link25"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18929

**Authors:** Katiya Fosdick, Michael McDonald, Gourav Khullar, Michael D. Gladders, Boris Häu{ß}ler, Cian Roche, Lindsey E. Bleem, Benjamin Floyd, Raven Gassis, Sebastian Grandis, Arnab Sarkar, Veronica Strazzullo, Taweewat Somboonpanyakul

**Abstract:** We introduce the Gini index (G) as a metric for studying the emergence of brightest cluster galaxies (BCGs) by quantifying the disparity in the light distribution of galaxy cluster cores. The Gini index collapses the skewness of the bright-end luminosity function to a single statistic, and provides a measure for the light dominance of the BCG over other cluster members that is more robust than the magnitude gap. We argue that the Gini index is far less sensitive to short-term changes in the cluster dynamical state than the magnitude gap and solves its known degeneracies by taking the entire cluster membership into account, making G a more sensitive metric to long-term BCG assembly. We apply the magnitude gap and Gini index to a progenitor-selected sample of 92 South Pole Telescope- and Planck-selected cluster cores at 0 < z < 1 with corresponding Hubble Space Telescope F110W and F200LP and Sloan Digital Sky Survey g and r photometry. Based on both parametric and empirical tests, we find no statistical evidence for evolution in G or $\rm M_{1,2}$ over 0 < z < 1, and find that BCGs have not grown significantly (2${\times}$) faster than the cluster population for z  1) in massive clusters. In general, we find that the Gini index is a valuable and relatively straightforward measurement, with well-controlled systematics, that could be applied to much larger samples to study BCG dominance and emergence.

**Abstract:** Ultralight dark matter (ULDM) can generate time-dependent gravitational perturbations, providing a potential signal for pulsar timing arrays (PTAs). In Galactic halos, the velocity dispersion of ULDM gives rise to finite spatial coherence, which influences the correlations between pulsar timing signals. In this work, we derive the timing-residual cross-correlations induced by rapid metric oscillations of spin-2 ULDM, allowing for general polarization statistics. Under equipartition among the five polarization modes, the intrinsic angular kernel contains monopole and quadrupole components and exhibits an angular profile closely resembling that of spin-1 ULDM, suggesting a potential degeneracy between the two ULDM candidates in PTA observations.

Zhang, LSST Dark Energy Science Collaboration

**Abstract:** We present the current implementation of an automated framework developed within the Legacy Survey of Space and Time Dark Energy Science Collaboration (LSST DESC) for galaxy cluster cosmology, in preparation for the first LSST data releases of the NSF-DOE Vera C. Rubin Observatory. The cluster cosmology pipeline integrates DESC-developed tools dedicated to galaxy/cluster catalog ingestion, cluster number count and weak lensing measurements, modeling, and parameter inference through posterior sampling, enabling reproducible end-to-end cluster cosmology analyses. We test and validate the pipeline using synthetic mock datasets and the LSST DESC cosmoDC2 simulation, performing a joint analysis of redMaPPer cluster abundance and stacked weak lensing profiles measured in bins of redshift and richness. We constrain two cosmological parameters, $\Omega_c$ and $\sigma_8$, along with six cluster mass-observable relation parameters. Our baseline analysis recovers the fiducial cosmoDC2 values of $\Omega_c$ and $\sigma_8$ within $0.51\sigma$ and $0.34\sigma$, respectively. We also showcase the flexibility of the overall pipeline by assessing the impact of fiducial cosmology and alternative modeling prescriptions on the parameter constraints. Lastly, we also discuss the current limitations of the pipeline and the developments needed for LSST data.

## 28. [Long-Term Radio Evolution of the Type Ia Supernova SN 2020eyj: Evidence for a Dense Helium-rich Circumstellar Shell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20669) <a id="link28"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20669

**Authors:** Zheng-Wei Liu, Xiaolong Yang, Huanyuan Shan, Xuefei Chen, Peng Jiang, Xiangcun Meng, Friedrich K. Roepke, Ryotaro Chiba, Jun Yang, Bo Zhang, Su Yao, Jingxiao Luo, Philipp Podsiadlowski, Liang-Duan Liu, Hailiang Chen, Shuai Zha, Wu Jiang, Rurong Chen, Jin-Tao Luo, De Wu, Lang Cui, Jiao Li, Xiang-Dong Li, Ye Li, Zhanwen Han

**Abstract:** The nature of progenitor systems of Type Ia supernovae (SNe Ia) and their explosion mechanism remain controversial. Radio observations provide a powerful probe of the interaction between SN ejecta and circumstellar material (CSM), offering important constraints on the mass-loss histories of their progenitor systems. We present extensive Jansky Very Large Array (JVLA) observations of SN 2020eyj at 1.5-10 GHz, tracing its radio emission to approximately 1300 days after discovery and revealing, for the first time, the long-term radio evolution of an SN Ia. The radio light curves of SN 2020eyj exhibit a relatively flat evolution at early epochs, followed by a gradual long-term decline, while its radio spectra show a power-law behavior with a low-frequency turnover. These characteristics appear to be consistent with synchrotron emission associated with shock interaction. Modeling the radio light curves favors a thick circumstellar shell with an inferred mass of approximately 0.3-0.4 solar masses over the simple wind-like configurations. Combined with the optical spectral signatures of SN 2020eyj, our radio observations provide strong evidence for substantial helium-rich mass loss from the progenitor system prior to explosion. The inferred CSM properties pose significant challenges to existing SN Ia progenitor models, although the ejection of a common envelope shortly before explosion may offer a plausible explanation.

## 29. [The Stability Frontier of Interacting Binary Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19026) <a id="link29"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.19026

**Authors:** Hisham Anwer

**Abstract:** We derive a conservation based dynamical stability frontier for interacting binary stars that distinguishes stationary mass transfer from the restoration of a perturbed contact state. We apply the formalism to conservative white dwarf binaries with direct impact accretion, coupling Roche overflow, stream angular momentum, gravitational radiation, and both stellar spin reservoirs. From the contact equations, we obtain the required orbital return torque, and an exact Jacobian identity yields the restoring gradient and an oscillatory stability boundary in the computed white dwarf domain. A dimensionally normalized quadrupolar tide reveals separate equilibrium fold and oscillatory thresholds, including coexisting stable and unstable stationary branches. Finite donor synchronization introduces an additional oscillatory stability frontier. The model recovers the classical Roche and gravitational radiation limits, while the calculated orbital period derivatives are consistent with published binary timing measurements within their combined observational and model uncertainties. These results specify the torque amplitude, restoring stiffness, and synchronization rate needed for local stability, providing quantitative targets for stellar tidal calculations and the interpretation of interacting binaries in the millihertz gravitational wave band.

## 30. [Lindblad and Corotation Resonances as the Common Origin of Moving Groups, Diagonal Ridges, and Velocity Waves in the Milky Way Disc](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19391) <a id="link30"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.19391

**Authors:** Tatiana A. Michtchenko, Willian Y. Nacafucasaco, Wilton S. Dias, Douglas A. Barros

**Abstract:** The Galactic disc is a complex dynamical system showing various kinematic structures, including moving groups (MGs), diagonal ridges (DRs), and velocity waves. To investigate their possible dynamical connection, we analyzed the phase-space distribution of stars from the extit{Gaia} DR3 catalog. We used angular momentum $L_z$ as a key dynamical tracer and examined stellar densities and velocities as functions of $L_z$. We identified stellar groups at specific $L_z$-values and associated them with extrema in the median radial velocity $\left$, known as a velocity wave. By cross-matching the extrema of the $\left$-wave with structures in the $V_R$--$L_z$ and $R$--$L_z$ planes, we established a clear connection between the velocity wave, the main MGs, and the DRs. A similar connection was partially identified for corrugations in the median vertical velocity $\left$. Both velocity waves are strongest near the Galactic mid-plane and decrease in amplitude with increasing vertical distance. Our results demonstrate that MGs, DRs, and velocity waves in the solar neighborhood are different projections of the same dynamical structures associated with Lindblad and corotation resonances. These resonances arise from coupling between the radial and azimuthal stellar motions and are particularly prominent in the thin Galactic disc. Numerical simulations including perturbations from the Galactic bar and spiral arms confirm these conclusions. Our findings indicate that the local stellar disc is currently evolving in a state of dynamical equilibrium. We also show that in-plane velocity oscillations must be considered when interpreting vertical stellar distributions, as they may produce artifacts in vertical profiles.

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21400

**Authors:** J. S. Urquhart (Kent), A. Y. Yang (NAOC), M. A. Thompson (Leeds, INAF-Catania), C. Koenig (MPIfR), D. Colombo (Argelander-Institut, MPIfR, INAF-Cagliari, Cologne-DYNAVERSE), W. -J. Kim (MPIfR), A. Giannetti (INAF-Bologna), A. Karska (MPIfR), F. Wyrowski (MPIfR), M. Figueira (National Centre for Nuclear Research, Poland), T. J. T. Moore (LJMU), L. K. Morgan (NRAO Green Bank Observatory), Enrique Vázquez-Semadeni (Instituto de Radioastronomía y Astrofísica - Mexico)

**Abstract:** We present the discovery and characterisation of G195.65$-$0.10 (also known as the $\lambda$-complex), a giant hub-filament system identified by the Outer Galaxy High Resolution Survey (OGHReS). The complex contains 61 spatially and kinematically coherent HiGAL clumps with a mean velocity of $V_{\rm LSR}=32.85$ km/s and a velocity dispersion between clumps of 1.97 km/s. A $Gaia$ parallax measurement of the central emission-line star MWC 137 associated with an embedded cluster gives a heliocentric distance of 5.2$^{+0.8}_{-0.6}$ kpc, placing the complex at a Galactocentric radius of 13.2 kpc. The molecular structure spans approximately $35\times63$ pc and contains $\sim 1.6\times10^{5}$ M$_\odot$ of molecular gas, including $\sim 5 \times 10^{4}$ M$_\odot$ in dense clumps. The molecular emission is organised into three prominent velocity-coherent filaments that converge on a dense central hub. Of the associated clumps, 16 are protostellar and 45 are gravitationally bound but currently starless. The protostellar clumps are significantly more concentrated towards the hub than the starless population, with median projected offsets of 6.65 and 15.83 pc, respectively. Moreover, the luminosity-to-mass ratio of the protostellar clumps decreases significantly with increasing distance from the hub, indicating that the most evolutionarily advanced sources are preferentially located in the central region. These results reveal a systematic concentration of dense gas and increasingly evolved star formation towards the hub, with organised large-scale gas motions throughout the filamentary network. The $\lambda$-complex therefore provides an unprecedented laboratory to understand hierarchical and high-mass star formation in the outer Galactic disc.

## 32. [A 3+1 formalism for self-similar expanding grids in computational relativistic and Newtonian magnetohydrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20865) <a id="link32"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20865

**Authors:** N. Bucciantini (INAF- Arcetri), L. Del Zanna (UniFi)

**Abstract:** Astrophysical systems frequently undergo spatial expansion during their evolution. Simulating these systems with standard conservative codes for hydrodynamics and magnetohydrodynamics using fixed grids is challenging, particularly when attempting to preserve high resolution across vast changes in physical scale. While moving or expanding meshes, or adapting mesh refinement, can address this issue, they introduce computational complexity and are notoriously difficult to implement robustly. We aim to overcome these barriers by providing an easily implementable strategy that empowers the community to achieve high-fidelity results and establishes expanding-grid approaches as a highly accessible standard practice. Leveraging the gauge freedom of the 3+1 formalism for MHD on curved manifolds, a foundation common to all general relativistic fluid dynamics codes, we demonstrate how an expanding coordinate system can be implemented purely through a simple modification of the underlying metric. This requires zero modifications to the numerical mesh or the core structure of the code, and the necessary addition or modification of metric terms can be easily accomplished even in Newtonian MHD. We apply this framework to standard test problems in both relativistic and Newtonian hydrodynamics and MHD, directly comparing the results of fixed and expanding coordinates. Our tests demonstrate the full consistency, accuracy, and robustness of this metric-based expansion approach, as the original conservative formulations is fully preserved. Through a simple coordinate transformation and a re-definition of the metric tensor, any general relativistic MHD code can be effortlessly transformed into an expanding-grid code. This approach elegantly guarantees both fundamental conservation properties and the solenoidal condition for the magnetic field, and can be readily extended to include non-ideal effects.

**Novelty:** 5

## 33. [Galaxy number counts in area distance space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20910) <a id="link33"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20910

**Authors:** Chris Clarkson, Roy Maartens, Stefano Zazzera

**Abstract:** We investigate how galaxy clustering changes when area distance is used as the radial coordinate. We derive the linear relativistic number counts for a flux-selected population and compare them with redshift and luminosity distance space. Source motion does not change the beam area at a fixed emission event, so area-distance clustering has no Kaiser distortion. Lensing changes both the angular and radial positions of sources, and can enhance area-distance clustering while weakening its correlation with redshift-space clustering. Observer motion shifts area-distance shells in the opposite direction to redshift and luminosity-distance shells, allowing their kinematic dipoles to point in opposite directions. A combination of the three count fields cancels their common intrinsic density and explicit evolution and magnification biases, leaving a signal dominated by velocities nearby and optical effects at larger distances. We relate this signal to galaxy-size magnification, so that its correlations can be inferred statistically without precise distances to individual galaxies.

**Authors:** Quanfeng Xu, Mi Chen, Renhao Ye, Siyuan Chen, Nan Li, Shiyin Shen

**Abstract:** Non-parametric statistics characterize complex galaxy morphologies but do not specify how to construct images that reproduce those statistics. We present Flow-VAE, which combines a variational autoencoder with a conditional normalizing flow to generate images conditioned on concentration, asymmetry, smoothness (CAS), Gini coefficient (G), and M20. A Gaussian mixture model completes unspecified descriptors and measures the training-distribution support of requested combinations. Using a dataset of 240,000 DESI Legacy Imaging Surveys r-band images, we evaluate fidelity by remeasuring morphological statistics from model outputs and comparing them with the inputs. With a tolerance of +/-10% of each descriptor's adopted range, descriptor-specific recovery rates are 96.62-99.97% for reconstruction and 87.81-99.83% for single-parameter generation among valid measurements. Generated descriptors show regression toward typical training values, while less-supported joint conditions produce broader residuals and systematic offsets. These results quantify the extent to which Flow-VAE can generate galaxy images consistent with prescribed non-parametric morphological statistics.

**Abstract:** Galactic chemical evolution (GCE) predicts large-scale variations in exoplanet compositions with host star elemental abundances, reflecting the Milky Way's history of star formation and elemental enrichment from stellar feedback. We demonstrate predicted trends using a sample of main sequence stars, and we show that these are generally reflected in the polluted WD sample. In particular, H-dominated WDs show preferentially larger iron mass fractions amongst younger and less dynamically excited systems. This trend is weaker amongst He-dominated WDs, and we show that this may be attributed to uncertainties in their observed phases of accretion. ## 37. [No Supersoft X-rays Before SN 2026ahvu: Constraints on a Potentially Prompt Type Ia Progenitor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21342) <a id="link37"></a>

**Authors:** Akos Bogdan, Tonima Ananna

**Abstract:** The nearby Type Ia supernova SN 2026ahvu offers a rare opportunity to probe its progenitor that may have evolved unusually rapidly. If the progenitor formed in either of the two young stellar clusters near the explosion site in NGC 1433, the delay from star formation to explosion could have been $\sim$100 Myr or less. The distance to NGC 1433 remains uncertain, with published estimates of 9.1 Mpc and 18.9 Mpc. Our preliminary fit to the rising ATLAS light curve yields a distance of $17.7^{+1.5}_{-1.4}$ Mpc. In the classical single-degenerate scenario, a white dwarf steadily burning hydrogen accreted from a non-degenerate companion can emit detectable supersoft X-rays before explosion. We search for this emission using two archival Chandra observations obtained before the explosion. The observations provide a combined exposure of 95.3 ks, with no 0.3-7 keV photons detected within 2" of the supernova position. For a 100 eV blackbody with Galactic foreground absorption, the 95\% bolometric luminosity limits are $6.3\times10^{37}\,\mathrm{erg\,s^{-1}}$ and $2.7\times10^{38}\,\mathrm{erg\,s^{-1}}$ at 9.1 Mpc and 18.9 Mpc, respectively. At both distances, the observations exclude a hot, compact white dwarf undergoing steady hydrogen burning if the absorption is limited to the Galactic foreground. However, cooler, expanded, or more heavily absorbed photospheres remain viable. Together with the pre-explosion non-detections for SN 2011fe and SN 2014J, our results reinforce the absence of luminous supersoft progenitors in nearby SNe Ia. This pattern is naturally consistent with double-degenerate explosions, although X-ray-faint single-degenerate configurations remain viable. Post-explosion X-ray observations can test for circumstellar interaction and help determine whether material around the progenitor could have obscured a luminous nuclear burning white dwarf.

**Abstract:** Context. We analyse projected rotational velocities ($v \sin i$) from high-resolution spectroscopy alongside 2-minute TESS photometry to establish empirical links between stellar rotation and pulsation parameters. The $v \sin i$ distribution is unimodal, with a mean of $120.3\text{ km s}^{-1}$ and peak probability at $113.8\text{ km s}^{-1}$. Fast rotators show a narrower range of photometric and pulsation amplitudes than slow rotators, and their lower mean stellar density ($\bar{\rho}$) cannot fully explain the frequency decrease. Calibrating Gaia's $v_{\rm broad}$ against our measurements removes its systematic offset, enabling use across larger $\delta$ Sct samples. Multi-spectrum comparisons reveal method discrepancies mainly for fast rotators ($v \sin i > 200\text{ km s}^{-1}$) and inter-spectrograph offsets up to $20\text{ km s}^{-1}$. Conclusions. We present the largest homogeneous high-resolution $v \sin i$ sample for bright $\delta$ Sct stars in the TESS era, showing that $v \sin i$ measurements in pulsating stars require careful treatment across instruments and velocity regimes.

**Abstract:** Context. Li-rich stars are rare, and only 16 are known in globular clusters (GCs), spanning evolutionary stages from the main sequence turnoff to the red giant branch. We aim to characterise two newly discovered Li-rich dwarf stars, 44606 and 58195, in the GC M 4 (NGC 6121) and investigate their origin among the formation channels proposed in the literature. The previously known Li-rich star in the cluster, namely 37934, was re-analyzed for comparison. Methods. We performed a standard abundance analysis based on 1D model atmospheres in local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) on high-resolution Ultraviolet and Visual Echelle Spectrograph (UVES). We measured the projected rotational velocity and searched for chromospheric activity. Results. We derive A(Li)= 2.58, 2.51 dex for stars 44606 and 58195, respectively, significantly different from the A(Li) distribution of normal dwarfs in M 4. For the known Li-rich star 37934, we obtain A(Li)= 3.01 dex, consistent with previous work. We estimate detailed abundances of 24 species (Li, C, N, O, Na, Mg, Al, Si, S, K, Ca, Sc, Ti, V, Cr, Mn, Fe, Co, Ni, Sr, Y, Zr, Ba, and Nd) in the three stars. None of our targets show detectable rotational broadening or chromospheric activity. Conclusions. The Li-rich stars analysed here are unevolved, ruling out in-situ Li production. Planet engulfment is very unlikely in GCs, and we detect no present-day rotation or activity. By exclusion, the most plausible remaining scenarios involve different types of interaction with a companion, including dynamical interactions, mass transfer, and mergers. ## 40. [Weak lensing data beyond flexion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21226) <a id="link40"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.21226

**Authors:** Ben D. Normann, Martin H. Enoksen, Nicolò Oreste Pinciroli Vago, Hans Georg Schaathun

**Abstract:** In weak lensing, shear and convergence are well studied and have provided a wealth of information about the distribution of matter in the universe. More recently, flexion -- the next term in the multipole expansion of the lensing potential, responsible for the characteristic banana-shaped distortion of lensed images -- has been shown to provide additional constraints on the mass distribution of lensing systems, particularly for galaxy clusters. Here we investigate whether weak-lensing information beyond flexion is similarly useful for lens-mass reconstruction. Using the roulette-amplitude formalism, we construct synthetic datasets for a single SIE lens and for SIE and SIS cluster lenses, with and without simulated Gaussian noise, and perform a least-squares reconstruction of the lens parameters using amplitudes truncated at third (flexion), fourth, and fifth order. As a proof of concept, we find that higher-order amplitudes do provide additional, useful information: the benefit is modest for a single lens, where flexion already constrains most parameters, but becomes substantial for cluster lenses, where the rotation and ellipticity are otherwise poorly recovered. The improvement is most pronounced in the presence of noise, where third-order reconstruction can become unreliable while higher-order amplitudes restore much of the lost accuracy. These results suggest that, as weak-lensing surveys grow in depth and precision, data beyond flexion could meaningfully improve mass reconstruction for the most complex and noisiest lensing systems, motivating further work using observational rather than synthetic data.

## 41. [Discovery of Ultra Fast Outflows in the Narrow Line Seyfert 1 Galaxy Mrk 110 with XMM-Newton and NuSTAR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19612) <a id="link41"></a>

**Authors:** O. Segura Montero, A. L. Longinotti, Y. Krongold, A. Marinucci, M. Guainazzi, S. Mathur, M. Gudiño, C. I. Victoria-Ceballos, A. Lara-DI, M. Santos-Lleó

**Abstract:** We present an X-ray analysis of XMM-Newton and NuSTAR spectra of the Narrow-Line Seyfert 1 (NLS1) galaxy Mrk 110. Our analysis reveals the intriguing presence of a multi-component ultra fast outflow (UFO) both in the grating spectrum and the NuSTAR data. The moderate UFO significance (3-4$\sigma$), in the grating and CCD data indicates a shallow absorber, consistently with the absence of previous such claims in this source. The two components (UFO1 and UFO2) in the grating spectrum show a fairly wide range of velocities (0.05-0.06c), as their respective ionization state (Log U = -1.43 and -1.82), however they share the same column density (Log N$_H$ $\sim$ 19.7). **Abstract:** Galactic bars are important tracers of disk-galaxy evolution, yet reported bar fractions vary among observational studies and between observations and cosmological simulations. TNG100 broadly agrees with SDSS over 10^10<~M*<~10^11.2Msun but shows a higher fbar at higher masses, whereas TNG50 remains lower than SDSS over most of10^10<~M*<~10^11.7Msun. Bulge-disk decompositions show fewer high-n bulges in TNG100 than in SDSS,particularly at high masses,but more in TNG50. These differences in bulge concentration may contribute to the different bar fractions. Bar length generally increases with stellar mass,and this trend depends little on the adopted bar-length definition. However, the outer ellipticity-decline estimator(R0.85max) yields systematically larger bar lengths than the ellipticity-peak estimator (R(eps,max)^bar). Massive TNG100 galaxies have more extended stellar disks than SDSS,which may contribute to their smaller relative bar lengths(Rbar/Re). Our results indicate that observational conditions,sample selection,and bar-identification methodology can affect the inferred bar fraction,emphasizing comparable observational conditions and analysis procedures in simulation-observation comparisons. Our SDSS measurements may underestimate the bar fraction relative to observations with higher spatial resolution.

In particular, the pattern observed at $R\lesssim5$ kpc can be associated with the influence of the Galactic bar. ## 45. [The kinematic response of RGB centroids and O--B stars to the Milky Way spiral structure: testing the solid-body rotation of the spiral pattern](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20973) <a id="link45"></a>

The periodic and smooth non-periodic velocity components are fitted simultaneously. For each sample, the amplitudes derived from the RGB centroid velocities and the individual O-B stellar velocities are used to calculate the relative frequency as a function of Galactocentric radius, $R$, and subsequently the pattern speed at different radii. The analysis is performed for both stellar populations and for two assumed numbers of spiral arms, $m=4$ and $m=5$, using either the amplitude ratio or the radial amplitude alone. None of the cases considered supports a constant pattern speed within the adopted Lin-Shu model. We regard the pattern speed inferred from the radial solution for a specified perturbing potential as our main result. The estimate based on the amplitude ratio provides a complementary result that is consistent with the main conclusion of a non-constant pattern speed, but is less reliable because of the azimuthal response.

## 47. [Black Hole Superradiance and the Gravitational Auger Effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18833) <a id="link47"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18833

**Authors:** Joshua W. Foster, Nicholas L. Rodd

**Abstract:** Black hole superradiance is a powerful probe of nature's light degrees of freedom. In the vicinity of a spinning black hole, it exponentially amplifies ultralight bosons from vacuum fluctuations to a macroscopic cloud of enormous occupation number. Observational superradiance effects - modified black-hole spins, gravitational-wave and coherent boson emission - are known to depend sensitively on bosonic self-interactions as induced by a quartic, $\lambda \phi^4$. Here we point out that purely gravitational interactions within the cloud significantly modify superradiance dynamics, through an analog of the Auger effect. Moreover, we identify nontrivial evolution of the superradiant levels beyond the first, driven by scattering into even higher levels and their radial overtones. For the QCD axion, the irreducible gravitational coupling rapidly dissipates the first superradiant level after it reaches saturation, through scalar emission and absorption by the black hole, while truncating the growth of higher levels. This can dramatically suppress the observability of continuous gravitational-wave signals or signatures in late binary inspirals. Black hole spin-down remains more robust, but for higher modes begins earlier and proceeds more slowly, requiring revision of existing limits.

**Novelty:** 6

## 48. [Cosmological Collider Signals from Fermion Bubble Loops with a Chemical Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20919) <a id="link48"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.20919

**Authors:** Chengcheng Han, Zhanhong Lei, Zhehan Qin, Wuzhou Yin

**Abstract:** We present the first systematic analytical calculation of the local and nonlocal cosmological collider (CC) signals generated by massive fermion bubble loops in the presence of an effective chemical potential during inflation. Working in the de Sitter approximation, we investigate the four-point correlator of inflaton perturbations in the single-squeezed limit. By combining CC cutting rules, late-time expansions, and partial Mellin-Barnes (MB) techniques, we derive analytical expressions for both local and nonlocal signals. In particular, we identify two distinct contributions to the local signal, arising from spectrum and integer poles and from ultraviolet poles of the loop-momentum integral. We further investigate the three-point limit, where the separately extracted local and nonlocal contributions develop apparent folded singularities. By reorganizing the MB representation, we demonstrate that these singularities cancel in the complete signal and obtain a convergent double-MB integral suitable for numerical evaluation. Independent calculations using complementary MB representations provide nontrivial consistency checks of our results. We find that the chemical potential can significantly enhance the oscillatory signal while increasing its logarithmic oscillation frequency. Our analytical results provide a foundation for further studies of chemical-potential-enhanced fermionic CC signals, including fermion triangle loops.

## 49. [Microcausality Bounds on Dispersion Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17407) <a id="link49"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17407

**Authors:** Paolo Creminelli, Michael Mancini, Warin Patrick McBlain

**Abstract:** Microcausality holds in any state, including those that spontaneously break Lorentz invariance, and requires the poles (and other singularities) of the retarded Green's function to satisfy $\mathrm{Im}\,\omega \le |\mathrm{Im}\,k|$ throughout the complex plane. Building on the work of Heller, Serantes, Spali\'nski and Withers, we study its consequences for dispersion relations that are linear at small $k$, like Goldstone bosons, assuming tree-level, non-dissipative dynamics. We show that microcausality is equivalent to requiring that $k \pm \omega(k)$ be typically real functions. This yields two-sided bounds on all higher-order coefficients of $\omega(k)$ in units of its radius of convergence $R$, the scale at which the linear Goldstone description breaks down. The optimal constraints are characterised by an infinite hierarchy of Toeplitz determinants. We derive analogous bounds on $\omega^2(k)$, whose radius of convergence $\tilde R$ marks the scale at which new states must appear. The bounds shrink to zero as the sound speed $c_s$ approaches 1: for a fixed higher-derivative correction, both $R$ and $\tilde R$ become parametrically smaller than naive dimensional analysis suggests. In particular, in the EFT of Inflation all higher-derivative quadratic operators must vanish as $c_s \to 1$. We test the bounds in several models: a $U(1)$ superfluid, light in a dielectric medium, photon propagation in a strong magnetic field, colour-flavour-locked quark matter, and a homogeneous solid. Finally, we extend the bounds to gapped modes.

**Abstract:** Particles with masses near the Hubble scale leave a characteristic pattern in the primordial bispectrum, turning inflation into a cosmological collider. We show that the template used in Planck analyses overestimates the amplitude by up to a factor of 1.8 and underestimates the mass, whereas our new template recovers both, as needed for current and upcoming CMB and galaxy surveys.
